=== age0 [military]  Recruiting Office How military service works Other ranks OR-1 → OR-9; officers OF-1 → OF-10 — most forces take officer cadets straight from school through selection and the military academy. Each service has its own trades; professionals (doctors, lawyers, nurses, engineers, clergy) can join as specialist officers. Regular service is your full-time job. Reservists train part-time alongside a civilian career, are paid for the days they serve, and can be mobilised; the law protects their civilian job while they're away. Pensions follow the Russian Empire's armed-forces scheme. 🇷🇺 Imperial Russian Army Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Officer · Regular Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) ⚓ Imperial Russian Navy Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Enlist · Reserve Officer · Regular Officer · Reserve Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) 🛡️ Mobilisation reserve Not open to you now Enlist Officer Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside)

=== age0 [school]  📚 Schooling in 1840 Most children leave school at 10 for work. Perhaps 7 children in ten never go to school at all. The universities don't admit women yet. University is for the well-off, and a few scholarship winners. 🧸 School School starts at age 5. 🏛️ College & Trade School Colleges, trade schools and certificate programs open at 17. Your school grades, activities and school will count. 📜 Diplomas No diplomas yet. 
=== age0 [licenses]  💼 Your Career Licences that stand between you and a job. Would open new jobs 🦷 Dental registration 📍 state-issued Take exam Needs age 22+ (years learning the work: no degrees then) opens Dentistry 🪪 Licenses & Certifications 0 active · 0 in training · 2 of 2 new credentials left this year How credentials work One registry for every license and certification. Employers, agencies and volunteer units pay for job-relevant credentials from their training budgets; police, fire, EMS, federal, railway employers run funded academies for their own hires and put you through the licences your job requires (failed exams are retaken the next year). Certifications for a promotion are yours to pursue. Convictions can suspend or revoke credentials. Exams can be failed — odds depend on your smarts (or fitness and health for physical tests). A prep course helps; failing a training program's final lets you retest without retraining for 2 years. Agency courses are open only to members, and pilots, drivers and academy recruits must pass a medical. State-issued licenses may need a transfer exam when you move. 🪪 Mine (0) ✅ Can pursue now (1) 🗂️ Browse by category ⚖️ Professional Licenses 🍎 Teaching qualification 📍 state-issued Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 6 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 29 · 81% pass odds

=== age0 [conditions]  🩺 Conditions No diagnosed conditions. Annual checkups catch silent ones (blood pressure, diabetes, early cancer). 🧠 Mind & Trauma Traumatic exposure 0 Combat, emergency calls, first-responder and CPS casework, prison and disasters build up exposure; it fades with time. High exposure can become a lasting case of nerves. Therapy works slowly and lasts; medication works faster with side effects; together they work best. In a crisis, send for the doctor or the minister.

=== age12 [military]  Recruiting Office How military service works Other ranks OR-1 → OR-9; officers OF-1 → OF-10 — most forces take officer cadets straight from school through selection and the military academy. Each service has its own trades; professionals (doctors, lawyers, nurses, engineers, clergy) can join as specialist officers. Regular service is your full-time job. Reservists train part-time alongside a civilian career, are paid for the days they serve, and can be mobilised; the law protects their civilian job while they're away. Pensions follow the Russian Empire's armed-forces scheme. 🇷🇺 Imperial Russian Army Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Officer · Regular Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) ⚓ Imperial Russian Navy Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Enlist · Reserve Officer · Regular Officer · Reserve Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) 🛡️ Mobilisation reserve Not open to you now Enlist Officer Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside)

=== age12 [licenses]  💼 Your Career Licences that stand between you and a job. Would open new jobs 🦷 Dental registration 📍 state-issued Take exam Needs age 22+ (years learning the work: no degrees then) opens Dentistry 🪪 Licenses & Certifications 0 active · 0 in training · 2 of 2 new credentials left this year How credentials work One registry for every license and certification. Employers, agencies and volunteer units pay for job-relevant credentials from their training budgets; police, fire, EMS, federal, railway employers run funded academies for their own hires and put you through the licences your job requires (failed exams are retaken the next year). Certifications for a promotion are yours to pursue. Convictions can suspend or revoke credentials. Exams can be failed — odds depend on your smarts (or fitness and health for physical tests). A prep course helps; failing a training program's final lets you retest without retraining for 2 years. Agency courses are open only to members, and pilots, drivers and academy recruits must pass a medical. State-issued licenses may need a transfer exam when you move. 🪪 Mine (0) ✅ Can pursue now (1) 🗂️ Browse by category ⚖️ Professional Licenses 🍎 Teaching qualification 📍 state-issued Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 6 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 25 · 85% pass odds

=== age12 [conditions]  🩺 Conditions No diagnosed conditions. Annual checkups catch silent ones (blood pressure, diabetes, early cancer). 🧠 Mind & Trauma Traumatic exposure 9 Combat, emergency calls, first-responder and CPS casework, prison and disasters build up exposure; it fades with time. High exposure can become a lasting case of nerves. Therapy works slowly and lasts; medication works faster with side effects; together they work best. In a crisis, send for the doctor or the minister.

=== age12 [legal]  ⚖️ Legal Status No current sentence. Serious crimes on your record here 0 Offences on your record here 0 Things that could still catch up with you 0 Serious offences bar most licensed professions, clearances, police reserves and military enlistment. Diplomatic immunity only shields you from host-country law. the Russian Empire (Bessarabia) executes most of those it condemns. 📜 Your Rights in 1852 🗳️ The vote : Not until 21. 👛 Your money : Marry, and your property and wages become your husband's (until reform). Credit needs a husband's signature (until 1975). 💍 Who you may marry : Two men or two women cannot marry. 💔 Divorce : Only on grounds: adultery, cruelty or desertion. 🧸 Custody : Children belong to their father: a divorced mother may lose them. 🏥 Abortion : A crime. ⚖️ The Law in 1852 Murder is punished by hanging, and few of the condemned are reprieved. 🗂️ Criminal Record Clean record.

=== age22 [life]  💡 How to Play ⏩ Press the green Age button (or Space) to live the next year. Decisions pop up as they happen. 🗂️ The buttons along the bottom are where you act: Occupation for school and work, Assets for money and home, Relationships for the people in your life, Activities for pastimes, health, travel and the law. The row at the top keeps your goals, honours, achievements and the news of the world. 🎓 Your late teens decide a lot: a degree, a trade, or a first job. Each has its own path. 🗓️ After each birthday, The Year in Review shows what changed. Your full story is below it, newest year first. === age22 [military]  Recruiting Office How military service works Other ranks OR-1 → OR-9; officers OF-1 → OF-10 — most forces take officer cadets straight from school through selection and the military academy. Each service has its own trades; professionals (doctors, lawyers, nurses, engineers, clergy) can join as specialist officers. Regular service is your full-time job. Reservists train part-time alongside a civilian career, are paid for the days they serve, and can be mobilised; the law protects their civilian job while they're away. Pensions follow the Russian Empire's armed-forces scheme. 🇷🇺 Imperial Russian Army Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Officer · Regular Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) ⚓ Imperial Russian Navy Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Enlist · Reserve Officer · Regular Officer · Reserve Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) 🛡️ Mobilisation reserve Not open to you now Enlist Officer Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside)

=== age22 [licenses]  💼 Your Career Licences that stand between you and a job. Would open new jobs 🦷 Dental registration 📍 state-issued Take exam Costs rub. 222 — more than your cash and credit opens Dentistry 🪪 Licenses & Certifications 0 active · 0 in training · 2 of 2 new credentials left this year How credentials work One registry for every license and certification. Employers, agencies and volunteer units pay for job-relevant credentials from their training budgets; police, fire, EMS, federal, railway employers run funded academies for their own hires and put you through the licences your job requires (failed exams are retaken the next year). Certifications for a promotion are yours to pursue. Convictions can suspend or revoke credentials. Exams can be failed — odds depend on your smarts (or fitness and health for physical tests). A prep course helps; failing a training program's final lets you retest without retraining for 2 years. Agency courses are open only to members, and pilots, drivers and academy recruits must pass a medical. State-issued licenses may need a transfer exam when you move. 🪪 Mine (0) ✅ Can pursue now (8) 🗂️ Browse by category 🚗 Driving 🐎 Hackney Carriage Driver's Licence Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 6 · 87% pass odds 🩺 Healthcare & EMS 🩺 Registered nurse (Nursing Council) 📍 state-issued Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 30 · 97% pass odds ⚖️ Professional Licenses 🍎 Teaching qualification 📍 state-issued Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 33 · 85% pass odds 🔧 Trades 📜 Indentures Served (Apprenticeship) Enroll (4 yr) 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 22 · 97% pass odds 📠 Telegraphist's Certificate (Morse) Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 7 · 87% pass odds 🏛️ Government 🍎 Teacher's Certificate (Pupil-Teacher and Training College) Enroll (2 yr) 📚 Prep rub. 9 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 44 · 76% pass odds 🍽️ Food, Hospitality & Service 🍺 Publican's Licence (Justices' Licence) Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 22 · 97% pass odds ⚓ Maritime 🧭 Mate's Certificate of Competency (Board of Trade) Take exam 📚 Prep rub. 7 · +12% odds 💳 You pay rub. 30 · 76% pass odds

=== age35 [military]  Recruiting Office How military service works Other ranks OR-1 → OR-9; officers OF-1 → OF-10 — most forces take officer cadets straight from school through selection and the military academy. Each service has its own trades; professionals (doctors, lawyers, nurses, engineers, clergy) can join as specialist officers. Regular service is your full-time job. Reservists train part-time alongside a civilian career, are paid for the days they serve, and can be mobilised; the law protects their civilian job while they're away. Pensions follow the Russian Empire's armed-forces scheme. 🇷🇺 Imperial Russian Army Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Officer · Regular Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) ⚓ Imperial Russian Navy Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Enlist · Reserve Officer · Regular Officer · Reserve Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) 🛡️ Mobilisation reserve Not open to you now Enlist Officer Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside)

=== age35 [licenses]  💼 Your Career Licences that stand between you and a job. Would open new jobs 🦷 Dental registration 📍 state-issued Take exam Costs rub. 204 — more than your cash and credit opens Dentistry 🪪 Licenses & Certifications 0 active · 0 in training · 2 of 2 new credentials left this year How credentials work One registry for every license and certification. Employers, agencies and volunteer units pay for job-relevant credentials from their training budgets; police, fire, EMS, federal, railway employers run funded academies for their own hires and put you through the licences your job requires (failed exams are retaken the next year). Certifications for a promotion are yours to pursue. Convictions can suspend or revoke credentials. Exams can be failed — odds depend on your smarts (or fitness and health for physical tests). A prep course helps; failing a training program's final lets you retest without retraining for 2 years. Agency courses are open only to members, and pilots, drivers and academy recruits must pass a medical. State-issued licenses may need a transfer exam when you move. 🪪 Mine (0) ✅ Can pursue now (0) 🗂️ Browse by category Nothing you can start right now — browse by category to see what each one needs.

=== age50 [military]  Recruiting Office How military service works Other ranks OR-1 → OR-9; officers OF-1 → OF-10 — most forces take officer cadets straight from school through selection and the military academy. Each service has its own trades; professionals (doctors, lawyers, nurses, engineers, clergy) can join as specialist officers. Regular service is your full-time job. Reservists train part-time alongside a civilian career, are paid for the days they serve, and can be mobilised; the law protects their civilian job while they're away. Pensions follow the Russian Empire's armed-forces scheme. 🇷🇺 Imperial Russian Army Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Officer · Regular Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) ⚓ Imperial Russian Navy Not open to you now Enlist · Regular Enlist · Reserve Officer · Regular Officer · Reserve Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) 🛡️ Mobilisation reserve Not open to you now Enlist Officer Enlisted: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside) · Officer: Women aren't enlisted yet (the nursing services aside)

=== age50 [licenses]  💼 Your Career Licences that stand between you and a job. Would open new jobs 🦷 Dental registration 📍 state-issued Take exam Costs rub. 159 — more than your cash and credit opens Dentistry 🪪 Licenses & Certifications 0 active · 0 in training · 2 of 2 new credentials left this year How credentials work One registry for every license and certification. Employers, agencies and volunteer units pay for job-relevant credentials from their training budgets; police, fire, EMS, federal, railway employers run funded academies for their own hires and put you through the licences your job requires (failed exams are retaken the next year). Certifications for a promotion are yours to pursue. Convictions can suspend or revoke credentials. Exams can be failed — odds depend on your smarts (or fitness and health for physical tests). A prep course helps; failing a training program's final lets you retest without retraining for 2 years. Agency courses are open only to members, and pilots, drivers and academy recruits must pass a medical. State-issued licenses may need a transfer exam when you move. 🪪 Mine (0) ✅ Can pursue now (0) 🗂️ Browse by category Nothing you can start right now — browse by category to see what each one needs.
